The header is read only for its <c2/> and is skipped up to <c3/>. Of the data records, only the <c4/> (satellite) and <c5/> (receiver or station) records of one clock, selected by name, are kept. Their first value field, the clock bias in seconds, is the phase. Continuation lines and the <c6/>, <c7/> and <c8/> records are skipped. Epochs are the record's calendar fields, as seconds from 1980-01-06 { "00" }:{ "00" }:{ "00" } in the file's own time system, with no conversion.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled, { "4" } partner-owned. Each capability carries one label in a machine-checked ledger: VALIDATED (an independent external oracle agrees: real data, an independent implementation or published reference vectors), MODELLED (internally consistent, and said out loud) or PARTNER (a hardware partner owns it). Among the checks: all { "666" } SGP4 (Simplified General Perturbations { "4" }) vectors of AIAA (American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics) 2006-6753 to { "4.12" } mm, and the Cowell force model to { "0.08" } m against Orekit { "12.2" }.

Hver funksjon har én merkelapp i et maskinkontrollert register: VALIDATED (en uavhengig ekstern referanse stemmer: reelle data, en uavhengig implementering eller publiserte referansevektorer), MODELLED (internt konsistent, og sagt høyt) eller PARTNER (en maskinvarepartner eier den). # Kshana Studio, Simple view, opening screen, titled What would you like to find out?, with the engine { "v0.38.0" } live and, in the top bar, three links: Training debrief, Interference map and Receiver trust. # How a run flows. A scenario TOML file (a kind, a seed and its parameters) goes into the engine, kshana { $version }, through the api::run_toml dispatch over { "87" } scenario kinds, deterministic from scenario, seed and engine version. The engine writes result.json, chart.svg, report.html and report.json, a table.csv for the kinds that define one, and on request SP3, CCSDS OMM and OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK and SigMF exports; a suite writes study.json and study.html. Those files feed Kshana Studio in the browser, an AI assistant through the kshana-mcp server, and continuous integration.
seg-76e3ec458441 = Hvordan en kjøring flyter. En scenario-TOML-fil (et slag, en seed og parametrene) går inn i motoren, kshana { $version }, gjennom api::run_toml-dispatchen over { "87" } scenarioslag, deterministisk ut fra scenario, seed og motorversjon. Motoren skriver result.json, chart.svg, report.html og report.json, en table.csv for slagene som definerer en, og på forespørsel eksport til SP3, CCSDS OMM og OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK og SigMF; en suite skriver study.json og study.html. Disse filene mater Kshana Studio i nettleseren, en AI-assistent gjennom kshana-mcp-serveren og kontinuerlig integrasjon.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check, and the label that follows. Continuous integration (CI) makes it impossible to call a capability VALIDATED without an independent external oracle.
seg-707e2cf795f6 = <b>{ "176" } av { "388" }</b> funksjoner validert mot uavhengige eksterne referanser; { "208" } ærlig merket modellert. Hver rad i verifikasjonsmatrisen navngir en funksjon, den uavhengige referansen den kontrolleres mot, testen som kjører kontrollen og merkelappen som følger. Kontinuerlig integrasjon (CI) gjør det umulig å kalle en funksjon VALIDATED uten en uavhengig ekstern referanse.

# How a capability earns its label. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check in continuous integration, and the label that follows. A row may be VALIDATED only with an independent external oracle; otherwise it is MODELLED, or PARTNER when a hardware partner owns it. Example: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbations, batch/sequential OD, oracle AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4 verification vectors, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, label VALIDATED. Live counts: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER of { "388" } rows. In all, { "176" } capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } more are honestly labelled MODELLED and { "4" } are PARTNER-owned.
seg-2f81cf391eae = Hvordan en funksjon får merkelappen sin. Hver rad i verifikasjonsmatrisen navngir en funksjon, den uavhengige referansen den kontrolleres mot, testen som kjører kontrollen i kontinuerlig integrasjon og merkelappen som følger. En rad kan bare være VALIDATED med en uavhengig ekstern referanse; ellers er den MODELLED, eller PARTNER når en maskinvarepartner eier den. Eksempel: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbasjoner, batch/sekvensiell OD, uavhengig referanse AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4-verifikasjonsvektorer, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, merkelapp VALIDATED. Levende tall: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER av { "388" } rader. Til sammen er { "176" } funksjoner validert mot uavhengige eksterne referanser; { "208" } til er ærlig merket MODELLED og { "4" } eies av en PARTNER.

# Kshana's architecture: one open engine at the centre, kshana { $version } under the AGPL-3.0, with api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over every kind, and the verification ledger of { "388" } capabilities ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Around it, every surface runs the same engine: Command line (cargo install kshana); Rust library (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker image (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains plugin (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Below it, Kshana Pro, a proprietary overlay that depends on the open engine as a library and never forks it, and adds no physical model.
seg-9d69cf43cd40 = Kshanas arkitektur: én åpen motor i sentrum, kshana { $version } under AGPL-3.0, med api::run_toml, en typet dispatch over hvert slag, og verifikasjonsregisteret med { "388" } funksjoner ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Rundt den kjører hver flate den samme motoren: kommandolinje (cargo install kshana); Rust-bibliotek (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP-server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker-avbildning (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains-programtillegg (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Under den ligger Kshana Pro, et proprietært overlegg som avhenger av den åpne motoren som bibliotek og aldri forgrener den, og som ikke legger til noen fysisk modell.

# Kshana's architecture in layers. Five front doors (command line, Python, JavaScript and WebAssembly, the MCP server and the JetBrains plugin) reach one api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over { "87" } kinds. Beneath it sit eight domain layers: time and frames, clocks and timing, inertial and fusion, GNSS and integrity, astrodynamics, LEO PNT, Moon, Mars and deep space, and missions and studies, on a shared core, all cross-referenced by the verification module, the machine-checked matrix of { "388" } capabilities that is the single source of truth for every label.
seg-9bc9a971bdb4 = Kshanas arkitektur i lag. Fem inngangsdører (kommandolinje, Python, JavaScript og WebAssembly, MCP-serveren og JetBrains-programtillegget) når én api::run_toml, en typet dispatch over { "87" } slag. Under den ligger åtte domenelag: tid og referansesystemer, klokker og tidssynkronisering, inertial og fusjon, GNSS og integritet, astrodynamikk, LEO PNT, Månen, Mars og dypt rom, og oppdrag og studier, på en felles kjerne, alle kryssreferert av verifikasjonsmodulen, den maskinkontrollerte matrisen med { "388" } funksjoner som er eneste kilde til sannhet for hver merkelapp.

# Three independent tools, pre-registered (0123cee1) before any fixture or oracle output existed, tolerances fixed then. ({ "1" }) ARAIM: Stanford MAAST for ARAIM { "2" } (commit ab70e2a3, BSD-3) mhss_raim_baseline_v5.m, run as a separate program under GNU Octave { "8.4" } with a driver-side unique() compatibility shim (disclosed, no algorithm change), at the ADD TOL_PL of { "0.05" } m: the Rust reproduces the ADD reference implementation on { "270" } real cases. Disclosed: the Kshana ADD path was written after the pre-registration, following the ADD as MAAST implements it; an independent hold-out by the reviewer ({ "270" } new user geometries and a second integrity support message, MAAST re-run against the frozen code) passed at the same bar. ({ "2" }) Snapshot RAIM: RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (BSD-2) estpos/valsol/raim_fde in a separate C harness for detection and exclusion; the slope PL from RTKLIB matinv/xyz2enu plus SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2, where both sides evaluate the same closed form (Brown; Parkinson and Axelrad), so the PL check covers the implementation, not the formula. ({ "3" }) SBAS: Stanford MAAST (commit 7d32b049) usr_vhpl, unmodified, on MAAST own recorded WAAS broadcasts: L1 levels within { "1e-4" } m after the K rescaling and an equal protected set. The same comparisons produced the findings stated in the capability: the uniform-sigma ARAIM functions are not ADD-conformant, and the L5 set equality fails on { "308" } pairs with no GEO in view (the levels on the { "3267" } pairs both tools protect agree to { "1e-13" } m). The DO-229E/DO-316 K-factors are transcribed constants and the real geometry is an input; neither is counted as an oracle. { "0.30" } revision: snapshot_raim protection levels moved from the radial to the geodetic local level (up to { "0.31" } m HPL and { "0.14" } m VPL at { "16" } deg N)

({ "1" }) ARAIM: Stanford MAAST for ARAIM { "2" } (commit ab70e2a3, BSD-3) mhss_raim_baseline_v5.m, kjørt som et eget program under GNU Octave { "8.4" } med et kompatibilitetsshim for unique() på driversiden (oppgitt, ingen algoritmeendring), ved ADD TOL_PL på { "0.05" } m: Rust-koden gjenskaper ADD-referanseimplementasjonen på { "270" } virkelige tilfeller. Oppgitt: Kshana sin ADD-bane ble skrevet etter forhåndsregistreringen og følger ADD slik MAAST implementerer den; et uavhengig tilbakeholdt datasett fra gjennomgangsansvarlig ({ "270" } nye brukergeometrier og en andre integritetsstøttemelding, MAAST kjørt på nytt mot den frosne koden) besto ved samme terskel. ({ "2" }) RAIM i et enkelt øyeblikk: RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (BSD-2) estpos/valsol/raim_fde i en egen C-harnisk for deteksjon og utelukkelse; stignings-PL fra RTKLIB matinv/xyz2enu pluss SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2, der begge sider evaluerer den samme lukkede formen (Brown; Parkinson og Axelrad), så PL-kontrollen dekker implementasjonen, ikke formelen. ({ "3" }) SBAS: Stanford MAAST (commit 7d32b049) usr_vhpl, uendret, på MAAST sine egne registrerte WAAS-kringkastinger: L1-nivåer innenfor { "1e-4" } m etter K-reskaleringen og et likt beskyttet sett. De samme sammenligningene ga funnene som står i funksjonen: ARAIM-funksjonene med uniform sigma er ikke i samsvar med ADD, og likheten av L5-settene svikter på { "308" } par uten GEO i sikte (nivåene på de { "3267" } parene begge verktøy beskytter stemmer til { "1e-13" } m). K-faktorene i DO-229E/DO-316 er transkriberte konstanter og den virkelige geometrien er en inndata; ingen av delene regnes som en uavhengig referanse. Revisjon { "0.30" }: beskyttelsesnivåene i snapshot_raim flyttet fra det radiale til det geodetiske lokale nivået (opptil { "0.31" } m HPL og { "0.14" } m VPL ved { "16" } grader N)

# Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. # Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision
seg-b6b8dec4dcff = To interne uavhengige referanser, intet eksternt datasett. ({ "1" }) Lukket-form tur-retur: den minste detekterbare biasen fremkommer ved å invertere halen til den ikke-sentrale kjikvadratfordelingen på ikke-sentraliteten (raim::pbias) og verifiseres ved å mate den resulterende ikke-sentraliteten tilbake gjennom raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf ved monitorens egen terskel, som må returnere P_md. ({ "2" }) Injeksjon mot analyse: en bias skrives inn i RF-estimatet for én akse og cross_raim::run_cross_raim kjøres på nytt, slik at statistikken som sammenlignes mot den analytiske ikke-sentraliteten er den monitoren beregnet, ikke en ny utledning. En seedet Monte Carlo av selve statistikken ligger i testene som en tredje kontroll; den er med vilje IKKE i rapporten, fordi et utvalgsestimat ville vært tregere og ikke reproduserbart bit for bit. ExternalDataset avslås: størrelsen er deteksjonsstyrken til DENNE monitoren ved DETTE scenarioets sigma-fordeling, og sigma-størrelsene MDB skaleres med er modellerte representative inngangsverdier, ikke målinger. ReferenceImpl ble også vurdert og avslått — Monte Carlo trekker den samme statistikken analysen beskriver, så den fanger transkripsjons- og koeffisientfeil men er ikke en uavhengig implementasjon av monitoren. { "0.30" } runde { "2" } (forhåndsregistrert 0135a9e4), blokkert (forblir MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq gjenskaper rapportens terskel (innenfor { "5.4e-12" }), MDB per akse, styrkekurven, kryssingsmultiplumet og rampens tid til deteksjon (innenfor { "5.3e-10" }) på fem konfigurasjoner; statistikkene for injiserte feil har ingen uavhengig referanse og sigma-størrelsene er modellerte inngangsverdier, så avgrensningen venter på en eierbeslutning

The GEOMETRY is checked against closed-form triangle trigonometry written as a different expression from the dot product under test. The CONTAINMENT of the real beam by the approximate cone is derived algebra (28.019/√η vs { "29.479" } degrees per λ/D), so the correction can only be non-positive for η ≤ { "0.9035" }. But the in-beam COUNTS themselves have no external oracle: no published table gives how many satellites of a Moonlight/LCNS-class shell hold a south-polar site inside a given dish's half-power beam, and the constellation is an illustrative public-source approximation, not a flown ephemeris. The measured disagreement at the documented working point — { "0" } links in beam under the real pattern against { "28" } under the approximation, −{ "2.33" } satellites per epoch, worst epoch { "3" }, on { "76" } evaluated links — is therefore a MODELLED finding about the approximation, pinned as a regression literal (the nearest row sits { "0.069" }° from either beam edge, four orders of magnitude above any last-digit disagreement), not an externally validated coverage number. Labelling the row ExternalDataset on the strength of the pattern's own external anchor would be borrowed validation, which is the move the matrix invariants exist to prevent. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered d9755d28), blocked (stays MODELLED): SPICE geometry and SciPy Bessel functions reproduce the off-boresight angle (worst { "3.0e-5" } deg), Airy gain and in-beam flags on { "6 336" } rows of flown-orbiter states and the documented working point; but on flown geometry the flags split by satellite identity (the nearest link is { "1.66" } deg from the beam edge), the { "5" } deg visibility is checked as a flag, not a numeric elevation, the symmetric-approximation flag applies the 31000/G rule of thumb to a validated angle without validating the rule, and the implied efficiencies have no oracle; the scoping awaits an owner decision

# An independent in-repo route. Every Jacobian row is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s evaluated from the state's own Earth-fixed and Moon-body-fixed coordinates rotated forward through the frame chain — a path that computes no derivative and shares no expression with the analytic partials — and the information matrices the two routes build agree to < { "1e-6" } of the largest diagonal. The linear-algebra kernel this composes (information_matrix, crlb, sym_eig) is separately externally anchored against numpy.linalg.eigh / numpy.linalg.inv in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs, and the delay observable carries lunar_vlbi's own delta-DOR far-field oracle; the row does not borrow either status. Closed-form identities checked here and labelled as such: σ² and 1/N scaling, and √(p/trace(M)) as an AM-HM lower bound the computed sigma cannot beat, which coincides with the equipartition value only on an isotropic geometry — which is why the measured ratio is exactly the anisotropy the assumption discarded. { "0.30" } external comparison (Library: NAIF SPICE CSPICE N0067 via spiceypy { "8.2.0" }, MIT; DE440, ITRF93 earth_latest_high_prec, MOON_PA), pre-registered ({ "50863551" }) together with an engine-side model-error sensitivity run: every Jacobian row is rebuilt by central differences of converged Newtonian light-time differences from SPICE geometry (the light-time equation is a short script over SPICE positions; SPICE carries the geometry), with the schedule rebuilt from SPICE elevations. The schedule, rank, defect, information spectrum, condition number, station sigmas, headline, trace bound, equipartition ratios, free-network null space, line-of-sight sweep and beacon declination agree within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.1" } deg, which sits above the engine known analytic-Moon error ({ "3.2e-3" } on sigmas, measured before the run). NumPy { "2.3.5" } redoes the linear algebra on the committed engine Jacobian to { "2e-13" } (P2). Validated as a Cramér-Rao bound for the stated reduced parameter set on the stated illustrative inputs: the Moon-centre ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held fixed in both legs, so it is not a real-campaign accuracy

# Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. MEASURED: campaign-derived translation sigma { "6.3975" } m against the injected-transform scenario's { "0.3125" } m recovery error ({ "20.5" }× tighter), while rotation and scale run the OTHER way at { "2.32" }× and { "13.5" }× looser — the injected path assumes one isotropic sigma and so spreads its error uniformly across seven parameters, which the delay observable does not. Rank 7/7 but condition { "3.8e5" }, with one direction { "99.57" } % pure translation-toward-Earth and forty times worse than any other. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM: that the campaign figure is right in absolute terms. It establishes that the figure now DERIVES from a schedule, a geometry and an error model rather than from a planted answer. Stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: no published lunar-VLBI campaign-plus-datum-covariance pair exists, the station network and delay sigma are ILLUSTRATIVE inputs (the beacon catalogue is sourced, the campaign is not), observations are treated as independent while a real session's troposphere and clock are correlated between nearby scans, and the ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held FIXED. { "0.30" } external comparison, a finding (stays MODELLED): with every beacon Jacobian row rebuilt from NAIF SPICE light times (DE440, ITRF93, MOON_ME; pre-registered { "50863551" }), the schedule, Helmert rank, datum sigmas (within { "4.7e-3" }), condition, weakest direction ({ "0.07" } deg), libration sweep and arc ordering agree on six scenarios within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.2" } / { "0.05" } deg. NumPy on the committed engine Jacobian agrees to { "3e-14" } where the stations are fixed. With the stations estimated it differs by { "6.8e-6" } against a pre-registered { "1e-9" }, whose condition-number premise was wrong (Helmert condition { "2.1e8" }; independent NumPy routes disagree by { "3e-6" }; a contribution from the engine { "1e-9" } pseudo-inverse fallback is not ruled out), so the row is not promoted on that pre-registration. { "0.36" } re-registration at a condition-scaled bar (pre-registered b887eb05; t = max({ "1e-12" }, { "10" } kappa { "2" }^-53) from the conditioning of the joint information and its Helmert reduction), a finding (stays MODELLED, NOT MET): step { "0" } rules the pseudo-inverse fallback OUT for the { "6.8e-6" } gap (no matrix upstream of the Helmert matrix falls back on the six original scenarios, tightest margin a factor { "2.6" }), the SPICE leg still holds on all six, and NumPy agrees within the bar on six of eight scenarios (default, the two arcs, the 3x delay sigma, the collinear defect and the fresh stations-fixed run on 2026-12-01; worst { "0.13" } of its bar). seg-8eedf0240350 = Lukkede identiteter og en uavhengig vei i depotet, uten at noen ekstern uavhengig referanse påstås. Helmert-designet — den eneste nye deriverte i modulen — utledes på nytt med sentral differanse av lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, en vei som ikke deler noe uttrykk med den analytiske formen. Akkumuleringen den bygger på er den til lunar-vlbi-fim, hvis Jacobian er differansederivert mot lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s der, og hvis lineæralgebrakjerne er separat eksternt forankret mot numpy i tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — denne raden låner ingen av disse statusene. Den fysiske uavhengige referansen er strukturell snarere enn numerisk: en radiofyrs partielle deriverte for forsinkelse er nærfeltsDIFFERANSEN mellom to nesten parallelle enhetsvektorer, så den dårligst bestemte translasjonsretningen MÅ være den kroppsfaste retningen mot Jorden, og testen hevder det beregnede svaret mot den separat målte retningen. # Internal consistency: the brute-force elevation scan, the hand-computed and orbit::dop reductions, and the closed-form Walker and geosynchronous geometry. Two-body orbits with an optional secular J2, a spherical body with the local vertical along the radius, geometric visibility only (no signal power, satellite health, terrain or third-body perturbation), and the relative phase between systems taken from different reference epochs, so it is not a snapshot of any date. { "0.30" } review, a finding (stays MODELLED): the presets meet the published one-sided DOP-availability floors (BeiDou OS Performance Standard { "3.0" }, Galileo OS SDD) at { "100" } %, but with the engine's PDOP doubled in a scratch copy they still meet them (worst point { "99.36" } %), so the floors cannot detect even a { "100" } % error in the quantity this row computes; tightening them after the run would be a post-hoc tolerance. Promotion needs a two-sided comparison of DOP values on identical geometry with a pre-set tolerance. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (tests/coverage_dop_full_oracle.rs, pre-registered add671d6), a finding (stays MODELLED): Orekit { "12.2" } positions, elevation, azimuth and ground tracks with gnss_lib_py { "1.0.4" } GDOP/PDOP/HDOP/VDOP (common clock) and a numpy inverse (one clock per constellation) agree on every bar on the Galileo, GPS, BeiDou, Galileo+GPS (one clock each) and a 1584-satellite Earth design (DOP within { "2e-14" } relative, identical fix, availability and visibility counts). On the Moon and Mars designs the elements, positions, visibility and tracks agree, but DOP exceeds the pre-set { "1e-9" } relative bar in cells whose largest PDOP is { "1434" } or more (up to { "8e-6" }), and { "4" } cells differ by one fix epoch on numerically singular geometry (engine pivot { "1e-10" } against numpy rank), so the row is not validated. { "0.30" } revisions: the TDOP/GDOP reference clock is now the lowest-numbered constellation in view (constellation-multi-gnss-coverage global GDOP max { "2.2236" } -> { "2.1205" }, mean { "1.122" } -> { "1.131" }), and per-constellation visible counts are summed as integers

WHAT IS NOT: the HTTP endpoint, delivery, timeouts and reconnection are not checked by an oracle, nor the Collector's prometheus receiver; the registry is read through accessors, so a fault in the registry state itself would not be caught; the OTLP 'committed output is current' check runs only with the otlp feature; the metric names and their usefulness to an operations centre are not validated; CEF and LEEF are not covered (see the next row).

Vår OTLP/HTTP JSON for det samme registeret leses av protobuf { "7.36.2" } json_format.Parse(ignore_unknown_fields=False) inn i ExportMetricsServiceRequest i opentelemetry-proto { "1.45.1" }, og hvert metrikknavn, hver beskrivelse, enhet, art, monotonisitet, aggregeringstidsmessighet, attributt, verdi, starttid og tid er helt lik registerets tilstand. Skjema { "1.3" } ({ "0.36" }), forhåndsregistrert (fb226057) før noe verktøy leste det: for en syntetisk skjema-1.3-strøm på 64 linjer (geometristatuser, DOP-er, etiketter og signaltap til stede, null, fraværende og ukjente; fradragsklasser; ikke-lesbare linjer) leser de samme to parserne geometriseriene (Prometheus kshana_trust_geometry*, OTLP kshana.geometry.*) og klasseetiketten på kshana_trust_reason_active nøyaktig slik registeret tilsier ({ "24" } familier og { "54" } samples; { "12" } metrikker og { "31" } punkter), promtool { "3.5.0" } check metrics avslutter med { "0" } uten lint-problem, og OpenTelemetry Collector contrib { "0.135.0" } godtar OTLP/HTTP JSON (status { "200" }, ingenting avvist) og filutskriveren registrerer forespørselen uendret (xval/telemetry-1.3, datastyrt: utgivelsesarkivene hentes med festet SHA-256 og utdataene deres committes). HVA SOM IKKE KONTROLLERES: HTTP-endepunktet, levering, tidsavbrudd og gjentilkobling er ikke kontrollert av noen uavhengig referanse, og heller ikke prometheus-mottakeren i Collector; registeret leses gjennom aksessorer, så en feil i selve registertilstanden ville ikke fanges; kontrollen 'committet utdata er gjeldende' for OTLP kjøres bare med otlp-featuren; metrikknavnene og nytten deres for et driftssenter er ikke validert; CEF og LEEF er ikke dekket (se neste rad).

# WHAT IS EXTERNALLY CHECKED, stated narrowly: against two independent open-source parsers (oracles: pycef { "1.11" } (PyPI, MIT); the Logstash CEF codec (logstash-codec-cef { "6.2.7" } in Logstash { "8.15.3" })), on a corpus of CEF lines this crate wrote ({ "157" } before amendment { "4" } below, { "170" } after it, { "176" } after amendment { "5" }): every band change and every epoch of a committed 84-line synthetic trust stream, plus adversarial text in hosts, reasons, time labels and gate names containing equals signs, backslashes, backslash-equals, pipes, line feeds, carriage returns, tabs, runs of spaces, multi-byte and astral characters and key lookalikes, extreme scores and offsets, every band with and without a previous band. pycef parses the lines whose header needs no escaping ({ "153" } before amendment { "4" }, { "166" } after it, { "172" } after amendment { "5" }) and recovers, exactly, the header fields, the severity, the exact extension key set and every value (numbers by f64 equality), after the CEF specification's unescape rules, which pycef does not apply and which scripts/gen_cef_ref.py applies. The Logstash codec, which unescapes by itself, parses every line ({ "157" }, then { "170" }, then { "176" }), meets the same bar, and also recovers four header fields that need escaping (a pipe, a backslash and an equals sign in the version string), which pycef's header pattern cannot read, so header escaping rests on the Logstash codec alone. The comparison bar was registered in commits { "5172e579" } (pycef) and 0a7598aa (Logstash) before either parser was run, and both met it at the first comparison. Five amendments are disclosed. Amendments ({ "1" }) to ({ "3" }) touched no comparison rule and no expected value: ({ "1" }) a corpus-size assertion was corrected from { "5" } to { "4" } band changes, a miscount, since the stream has { "4" }; ({ "2" }) the Logstash runner's pipeline delimiter wiring was fixed after a first attempt that produced no events; ({ "3" }) the trust score and epoch offset were moved from the CEF integer fields cn1 and cn2 to the floating-point fields cfp1 and cfp2, a wire-format change made in this release, after which the corpus was regenerated and both oracles were re-run with the bar unchanged and met it again. Amendment ({ "4" }), { "0.36" }, extended the corpus and the expected key set, registered in d6331429 before either parser ran on the new lines: schema { "1.3" } inputs add the satellite geometry in the custom fields cs6 (label geometry: status, then ';' and the deduction classes), cfp3 (geometryHdop) and cfp4 (geometryPdop); { "13" } geometry cases (the corpus to { "170" } lines) and the three new keys; both met the bar, otherwise unchanged, at the first comparison (pycef { "166" } of { "170" } lines parsed, the { "4" } tier { "2" } header cases as before; Logstash { "170" } of { "170" }). Amendment ({ "5" }), { "0.36" }, appended { "6" } geometry-label cases (the corpus to { "176" } lines), registered in 9a8e4fa9 before either parser ran on them: from inputs that carry the geometry label, cs6 gains a third field (';' and the label) and cn1 (geometryUsedSatellites) the satellites used; the Logstash codec's mapping table was read first (no flexString fields, hence cs6 rather than flexString1); both met the bar at the first comparison (pycef { "172" } of { "176" }, Logstash { "176" } of { "176" }), and every earlier reference entry was reproduced unchanged. WHAT IS NOT: neither parser type-checks the cfp values (both keep them as text), so the use of the floating-point fields rests on the CEF dictionary rather than on an oracle; no vendor SIEM was run; the extension key names and their mapping are this crate's choice, and the parsers show only that they are read back as written; the RFC { "5424" } envelope, delivery, timeouts and reconnection are not covered; pycef is a small parser last released in { "2020" }, and the Logstash codec is the more substantial oracle.

# checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. All { "15" } are predicted visible and the single pair's residual is -0.06 dB; nothing is claimed

Bak aperturen bruker modellen nå et flatt gulv for baklobe, oppgitt som en grense i stedet for en oppdiktet mønsterform. # checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision

({ "1" }) Rundtur i lukket form: den minste detekterbare biasen fremkommer ved å invertere halen til den ikke-sentrale kjikvadratfordelingen på ikke-sentraliteten (raim::pbias) og kontrolleres ved å mate den resulterende ikke-sentraliteten tilbake gjennom raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf ved monitorens egen terskel, som må returnere P_md. ({ "2" }) Injeksjon mot analyse: en bias skrives inn i RF-estimatet for én akse og cross_raim::run_cross_raim kjøres på nytt, så statistikken som sammenlignes med den analytiske ikke-sentraliteten er den monitoren beregnet, ikke en ny utledning. En seedet Monte Carlo av selve statistikken er med i testene som en tredje kontroll; den er bevisst IKKE i rapporten, fordi et utvalgsestimat ville vært tregere og ikke reproduserbart bit for bit. ExternalDataset er avvist: størrelsen er deteksjonskraften til DENNE monitoren ved DETTE scenarioets sigma-fordeling, og sigma-størrelsene som MDB skaleres med er modellerte representative inndata, ikke målinger. ReferenceImpl ble også vurdert og avvist — Monte Carlo trekker den samme statistikken som analysen beskriver, så den fanger opp transkripsjons- og koeffisientfeil men er ikke en uavhengig implementasjon av monitoren. { "0.30" } runde { "2" } (forhåndsregistrert 0135a9e4), blokkert (forblir MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduserer rapportens terskel (innenfor { "5.4e-12" }), MDB per akse, effektkurve, krysningsmultiplum og rampetid til deteksjon (innenfor { "5.3e-10" }) på fem konfigurasjoner; statistikkene for injiserte feil har ingen uavhengig referanse og sigma-størrelsene er modellerte inndata, så avgrensningen venter på en eierbeslutning

# checked against a separate implementation in this same codebase — independent of the unit under test, but not externally authoritative — Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. MEASURED: campaign-derived translation sigma { "6.3975" } m against the injected-transform scenario's { "0.3125" } m recovery error ({ "20.5" }× tighter), while rotation and scale run the OTHER way at { "2.32" }× and { "13.5" }× looser — the injected path assumes one isotropic sigma and so spreads its error uniformly across seven parameters, which the delay observable does not. Rank 7/7 but condition { "3.8e5" }, with one direction { "99.57" } % pure translation-toward-Earth and forty times worse than any other. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM: that the campaign figure is right in absolute terms. It establishes that the figure now DERIVES from a schedule, a geometry and an error model rather than from a planted answer. Stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: no published lunar-VLBI campaign-plus-datum-covariance pair exists, the station network and delay sigma are ILLUSTRATIVE inputs (the beacon catalogue is sourced, the campaign is not), observations are treated as independent while a real session's troposphere and clock are correlated between nearby scans, and the ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held FIXED. { "0.30" } external comparison, a finding (stays MODELLED): with every beacon Jacobian row rebuilt from NAIF SPICE light times (DE440, ITRF93, MOON_ME; pre-registered { "50863551" }), the schedule, Helmert rank, datum sigmas (within { "4.7e-3" }), condition, weakest direction ({ "0.07" } deg), libration sweep and arc ordering agree on six scenarios within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.2" } / { "0.05" } deg. NumPy on the committed engine Jacobian agrees to { "3e-14" } where the stations are fixed. With the stations estimated it differs by { "6.8e-6" } against a pre-registered { "1e-9" }, whose condition-number premise was wrong (Helmert condition { "2.1e8" }; independent NumPy routes disagree by { "3e-6" }; a contribution from the engine { "1e-9" } pseudo-inverse fallback is not ruled out), so the row is not promoted on that pre-registration. { "0.36" } re-registration at a condition-scaled bar (pre-registered b887eb05; t = max({ "1e-12" }, { "10" } kappa { "2" }^-53) from the conditioning of the joint information and its Helmert reduction), a finding (stays MODELLED, NOT MET): step { "0" } rules the pseudo-inverse fallback OUT for the { "6.8e-6" } gap (no matrix upstream of the Helmert matrix falls back on the six original scenarios, tightest margin a factor { "2.6" }), the SPICE leg still holds on all six, and NumPy agrees within the bar on six of eight scenarios (default, the two arcs, the 3x delay sigma, the collinear defect and the fresh stations-fixed run on 2026-12-01; worst { "0.13" } of its bar). It fails on stations_estimated (datum sigmas up to { "6.8e-6" }, condition { "9.1e-6" }, independence-discarded norm { "3.5e-6" }, against t = { "5.9e-7" }), and on the fresh date the south-pole beacon has no observation, so the beacon information is rank-deficient (pseudo-inverse fallback upstream of the Helmert matrix in both fresh scenarios) and the unchanged NumPy oracle cannot evaluate stations_estimated_fresh (singular matrix). # <b>What the observatory does instead.</b> The question the almanac comparison answers is "could the constellation geometry alone explain this low accuracy?". The base method answers a related question without an almanac: a low share must stand out against the same day's background across the region and against the aircraft's own good reports elsewhere. That is a different and weaker substitute: it cannot say that a quiet-geometry hour was degraded where the day's background is also low. An almanac-HDOP scorer is <b>not implemented</b>. It is an open item, to be built from the paper's full text and the public GPS almanac and pre-registered before it is run; until then it is cited, not claimed.
seg-4f8471649a07 = <b>Hva observatoriet gjør i stedet.</b> Spørsmålet almanakksammenligningen besvarer er «kunne konstellasjonsgeometrien alene forklare denne lave nøyaktigheten?». Grunnmetoden besvarer et beslektet spørsmål uten almanakk: en lav andel må skille seg ut mot samme dags bakgrunn i regionen og mot flyets egne gode rapporter andre steder. Det er en annen og svakere erstatning: den kan ikke si at en time med rolig geometri var degradert der dagens bakgrunn også er lav. En almanakk-HDOP-skårer er <b>ikke implementert</b>. Det er et åpent punkt, som skal bygges fra artikkelens fulltekst og den offentlige GPS-almanakken og forhåndsregistreres før den kjøres; inntil da siteres den, den påstås ikke.
